These ordinarily were of the sect of the Pharisees The Prevailing Iniq. of those at that day was self righteousness [^003-note1]

The subject the Apostle had insisted on throughout the Preceding Chap. was the Extraordinary 1 The Things here Compared . viz The viz these two The extraordinary Gifts of the sp. sp. that implied Inspiration or immediate re & the Graces of the sp. By [^011-note1] Gifts of the sp. I mean those that implied consisted in or implied Inspiration th Inspiration or immediate Revelation . These had been the were the subject the Apostle had insisted on throughout the foregoing Chap. & then The same are spoken of in this Chap. & compared having in that Chap. insisted He continues on the extraordinary Gifts He proceeds in this Chap. to compare those [-] Gifts of the sp. with the Grace of the sp. to shew how far the latter exceeds the former which comparison is introduced by the last words in the preceding Chap[^011-note2] But Covet ---------------

_______________________

The extraordinary Gifts Here mentioned in the Text are Prophesying Tongues & Knowledge The same were spoken of in the two first verses . ---- The same also had been spoken of in the foregoing Chap. 8 . 9, 10 verses. what is called Knowledge here was a certain extraordinary Gift -----

These are the Extraordinary Gifts that are spoken of in this the Text

The Chh. of X may be said to pass under such a change from a state of minority to a state of maturity two ways 1 By the saints passing from a temporal that state of Imperfection They are in here in this O to a state of Pe glorious Perfection in another O The saints while here are but as little Chil.

Thus the Chh. of G. while under the old Testament is represented as being in a state of Childhood compared with what the state it is brought into under the new Gal. 4. at the Beginning. so the Xtian[^011-note9] Church while [-] in its first beginning before the new dispensation of the Cov of Grace is per fectly perfected & settle it may is as it were in its Infancy or Childhood in comparison of what it is afterwards when the new old dispensa. is Perfectly abolished & the new dispensation complea fully established. & all things belonging to this new & more glo. state compleated & settled. & Tis evident that the Apostle has Respect to the transition of the Chh.[^011-note10] from a state of Child hood in this latter Respect when he speaks of the extraordinary Gifts of Prophecy --- ceasing . & faith hope & Charity abiding after they have ceased. for -- don't abide in Heaven.

whatever may be said of such societies that are founde the Fou as men form Thems. on the Founda proceeding on the Founddation of the Law & Light of[^014-note29] nature [^014-note30] but does by no means hold true of those societies that are founded wholly on divine Institu. if men form Thems. into societies of their own according having no other guide or Founda. but only the light of Law of nature & the light of nature for their then they have no Rule to proceed by for then They have all an equal [-] Power for nature gives one man no more power than another excepting in Families But with Regardto such societies as have their very being wholly from divine Institution as it is with the Xtian Chh. this is [^014-note31] not &[^014-note32] cannot be the Case for for if the very Being of the society be founded on divine Institution then so [-] is the Constitution of the society . The Gospel [--] is all over a dispensa entirely above nature. The Cov of Gra The Priviledges of the Cov. of Grace are none of them founded in nature right. we have no claim by nature to any we but the very being of the Christian Chh is built on the Cov. of Gr. we have no right or claim by nature to any one Gospel Priv. & theref. no right to any Chh. Grace & pleasure of G. & hithe Revelation of his sov. will all that belongs to the Gospel or a Gospel society has its founda. here & not in natural Rights & Theref. they that make this objec tion from the natural rights of the Human nature entirely mistake the matter G The meer pleasure & sovereign will of G. as Revealed in the Gosp. is the whole founda. of the being of a Chh. & fo the same is that which regulates it
